#### Pruning

- Tool-result summarisation: off by default (`GOOSE_TOOL_PAIR_SUMMARIZATION`, undocumented). When on, after compaction an operation replaces old tool request/response pairs with one-line LLM summaries. Cutoff is `GOOSE_TOOL_CALL_CUTOFF` or `clamp(3 x (limit x threshold) / 20,000, 10, 500)` tool calls (15 at a 128K window, 24 at 200K, 120 at 1M; the formula was recomputed by a reviewer, the examples were not). When eligible calls (all minus the current turn) exceed cutoff plus 10, the oldest 10 are summarised, one LLM call each, in line. The original pair is hidden from the agent. A failed per-pair summary is logged and skipped. Trust: checked in review. https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/context_mgmt/mod.rs#L55-L59 , https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/context_mgmt/mod.rs#L414-L422 , https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/state_machine/ops_tool_pair_compaction.rs#L134-L173
- Spill at record time: any text block longer than `GOOSE_MAX_TOOL_RESPONSE_SIZE` (default 200,000 characters, about 50,000 tokens) is written to a `goose_mcp_response_*.txt` temp file (kept) and replaced by a message naming the file; if writing fails the full text is returned with a warning. Cap is per text block. Trust: checked in review. https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/large_response_handler.rs#L5-L77
- Shell: each of stdout and stderr limited to 2,000 lines or 50,000 bytes; over the limit the full text goes to a per-tool temp dir (8 rotating slots, so the 9th overwrites the 1st) and the model gets the last 50 lines (capped to 10,000 bytes) plus a notice naming the file. Output is first held fully in memory. Trust: checked in review. https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/platform_extensions/developer/shell.rs#L161-L166 , https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/platform_extensions/developer/shell.rs#L747-L787
- A `summarize` platform tool loads files (100 KB each, 1 MiB total, respects `.gitignore`) and makes one LLM call on the session model so raw contents never enter the parent's context. Trust: unchecked. https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/platform_extensions/summarize.rs#L22-L23

#### Log record

- `replace_conversation` deletes and re-inserts all messages in one transaction, but compaction uses `save_compacted_conversation`: it matches messages by `message_id`, updates only the `agent_visible` flag in `metadata_json` on existing rows and inserts the new summary rows. Old messages stay stored, user-visible and hidden from the model. A compacted message without an id makes the save fail ("compacted conversation message has no id"). There is no per-message record of why or when compaction ran other than the `compaction_summary` flag on the summary message. There is no guard against a concurrent writer in another process. A failed summary leaves the stored conversation unchanged (the save happens after the summary exists; inferred from the pipeline order). Trust: read from the definition, unchecked. https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/session/session_manager.rs#L2323-L2362 , https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/session/session_manager.rs#L2375-L2433 , https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/session/session_manager.rs#L2385-L2389 , https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose-provider-types/src/conversation/message.rs#L831-L833

#### Instruction files

- Context filenames default to `.goosehints` and `AGENTS.md` (`CONTEXT_FILE_NAMES` overrides). Both are loaded and concatenated, never either/or. Global first: `<config_dir>/.goosehints`, `<config_dir>/AGENTS.md`, then `~/.agents/AGENTS.md` (only if AGENTS.md is in the list). Then local files for every directory from the git root down to the working directory (root first, cwd last), in filename order per directory. Output is `### Global Hints ... ### Project Hints ...`, inserted as the `hints` extra. `@path` tokens expand inline within an import boundary (git root or cwd), depth at most 3, 64 reference operations and 1 MiB expanded; a file over 128 KiB gets no reference parsing. The main file is read whole with no size cap. Unsafe paths (symlink escapes, `.git` internals) are skipped with a warning. Loaded for every session including sub-agents, not gated on the Developer extension. Trust: checked in review. https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/hints/load_hints.rs#L10-L24 , https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/hints/load_hints.rs#L232-L311 , https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/hints/import_files.rs#L14-L17 , https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/hints/import_files.rs#L294-L305 , https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/prompt_manager.rs#L90-L100
- Trust: no trust prompt; a cloned repo's `.goosehints` goes straight into the system prompt, with only the Unicode-tag strip as defence. Whether the desktop UI shows a prompt was not searched (the reviewer notes the negative is a search of Rust code). Trust: checked in review.
- Subdirectory hints: each time a prompt is built, the operation replays every agent-visible tool request, collects `path` arguments and path-like tokens from `command` arguments, and for each directory strictly inside the working directory loads that directory's hint files plus ancestors below the working directory, each as an extra `subdir_hints:<dir>`. Recomputed from history, so they persist for the session but drop out after compaction hides the triggering messages until the model touches those paths again (that consequence is inferred). Trust: read from the definition; compaction interaction inferred. https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/hints/load_hints.rs#L26-L103 , https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/hints/load_hints.rs#L115-L168 , https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/state_machine/ops_toolcalling.rs#L931-L950

#### Memory and session search

- Built-in Memory extension (plain tool server, text files). Tools `remember_memory`, `retrieve_memories`, `remove_memory_category`, `remove_specific_memory`. Storage is `<category>.txt`: local at `<working_dir>/.goose/memory/`, global at `<config_dir>/memory/`. `remember` appends `# tags` then the text. At construction all global memories are loaded into the extension's instructions string; local memories are not pre-loaded (they need `retrieve_memories`). The snapshot is taken at construction, so memories saved mid-session reach the prompt only in a new session. Category names are validated as single filename components. No size limit is described in the input.
- Approval: the instructions tell the model "Always confirm with the user before saving", but nothing in the tool enforces it. Anything the model writes into a memory file is re-injected into later system prompts, a persistence channel for prompt injection. Who writes: the chat model, at its own discretion. No background review, no curator, no rollback described. Trust: checked in review ("Memory extension prompt-only confirmation: supported"). https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose-mcp/src/memory/mod.rs#L118-L172 , https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose-mcp/src/memory/mod.rs#L184-L215 , https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose-mcp/src/memory/mod.rs#L260-L285
- Session search: the input files do not describe content search over sessions in goose. A grep of `goose.sessions.md` found no search feature; there is also no retention or pruning job for sessions (absence confirmed by a reviewer).

### Cline

Source: `cline.agents-sessions.md` (25 of 37 findings checked in review: 20 supported, 5 partly and corrected in place, 0 wrong) and `cline.safety.md` (its compaction row says "not read"). Pinned commit `500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663`. The code is the new TypeScript SDK (`sdk/packages/{shared,llms,agents,core}`); the old per-model-family prompts of the extension are gone.

#### Prompt assembly and cache

- There is one prompt builder, `buildClineSystemPrompt`. It picks one of two constant templates (`ACT` for plan and act modes, `YOLO` for yolo) and fills slots by string replacement: `{{PLATFORM_NAME}}`, `{{CWD}}`, `{{CURRENT_DATE}}`, `{{IDE_NAME}}`, `{{CLINE_RULES}}`, `{{CLINE_METADATA}}`. Plan mode is the ACT template plus a plan-mode block appended into the rules slot. A caller-supplied `overridePrompt` replaces the whole template. Nothing branches on model or provider except the metadata slot. Builder: https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/shared/src/prompt/cline.ts#L152-L213 ; templates: https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/shared/src/prompt/system/act.ts#L1-L36 and https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/shared/src/prompt/system/yolo.ts#L1-L40 . Label: traced end to end (checked in review as part of the finding set).
- The git remote, branch and commit block (`{{CLINE_METADATA}}`) is filled only when `isClineProvider(providerId)` is true, so it goes to Cline's own gateway and to no other provider; remote URLs have credentials stripped first. https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/shared/src/prompt/cline.ts#L205-L209 and https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/shared/src/prompt/cline.ts#L55-L77 . If prompt building throws, VS Code falls back to a one-line prompt.
- The mode is carried twice. The plan contract is appended to the system prompt, and every user message is wrapped as `<user_input mode="...">`, with a `<mode_notice>` element at the exact turn where the user toggled modes. `MODE_TAG_INSTRUCTIONS` tells the model the newest message's mode governs. YOLO omits all of it. This keeps the mode change out of the system prompt prefix. https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/shared/src/prompt/format.ts#L5-L10 and https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/shared/src/prompt/format.ts#L41-L46 . Label: read the definition.
- Tool set per mode and model: mode presets (act: read, search, bash, web fetch, editor, skills, ask, spawn, teams; plan: same minus editor; yolo: read, bash, editor, `submit_and_exit`). A routing table swaps `editor` for `apply_patch` when the provider is `openai-native` or the model id contains `codex` or `gpt` (case-insensitive substring). Mode switch rebuilds the runtime and tool list. https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/tools/model-tool-routing.ts#L60-L75 . Label: traced end to end.
- Skills appear only as names inside the description of the `skills` tool; the body is delivered when the model calls the tool. https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/tools/definitions.ts#L809-L821
- Cache-related behaviour in the message builder: a re-read of the same file range rewrites older copies to `[outdated - see the latest file content]`, batched once 64 KB can be reclaimed "to avoid breaking prefix caches". Rules are re-read from a file watcher on each request, which can change the prefix (the finding says to decide this deliberately; no cache measurement was made). Label: read the definition; cache effect inferred.

#### Instruction files

- Rules are rendered as `# Rules` with `## <name>` sections in stable name order by a rule registered with the extension API. Search order: workspace `AGENTS.md`, `.clinerules`, `.cline/rules`, global `AGENTS.md`, then global rule directories. No size limit on rule text was found. https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/runtime/safety/rules.ts#L10-L21 and https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/shared/src/storage/paths.ts#L586-L616 . Label: traced end to end for rendering; size-limit absence unchecked.
- Conditional rules (frontmatter `paths:` globs) exist only in the legacy VS Code module; the SDK renderer includes every enabled rule and no caller of the evaluator was found on that path. https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/vscode/src/core/context/instructions/user-instructions/rule-conditionals.ts#L1-L10 . Label: inferred (search for callers).
- Trust: workspace plugins, hooks, skills and agents (`<workspace>/.cline/plugins`, `.cline/hooks`, `.clinerules/hooks`) load with no trust prompt; a search for "trust" in the loaders found nothing. Cline's own vendor-neutral "Agent Plugins" lane scans only the home directory so that opening a repository cannot activate repository-controlled MCP servers. https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/shared/src/storage/paths.ts#L495-L509 . Label: inferred (checked in review). The finding is about plugins and hooks; the rules files themselves are also read from the workspace with no trust step mentioned.

#### Compaction

- Gate: opt-in in the core, on by default in the CLI (`agentic`), and driven by the "auto condense" setting in VS Code. https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/cli/src/utils/compaction-mode.ts#L33-L45 .
- Trigger and measurement: a hook runs before each model request. It estimates request tokens at about 3 characters per token plus image costs, derives the model's input limit from model info, and compacts when the estimate reaches 90% of that limit. When the model reports only a context window and no `maxInputTokens`, the limit is itself 0.9 of the window, so the effective trigger is about 81% of the window (corrected in review). https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/context/compaction-shared.ts#L65-L82 ; trigger https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/context/compaction.ts#L330-L372 .
- Self-correcting estimator: if the provider's real count for the previous request exceeded the estimate, the budget is scaled down by that ratio, capped at 4x. When the model's limit is unknown, automatic compaction does not run; manual compaction and overflow recovery fall back to a 128,000-token budget. Target after compaction: 70% of the trigger, or 50% of the input limit for conversations of 5 or more user and assistant pairs on models whose output cap is below their input limit. https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/context/compaction.ts#L211-L230 . Label: traced end to end (corrected in review for the 81% point).
- `agentic` strategy: find a cut index preserving the most recent 20,000 estimated tokens (never cutting through the last typed user turn or a tool call/result pair); summarise everything before it with one model call; replace that prefix with one user message `Context summary:` carrying structured metadata. The summariser uses the active provider and model unless a separate summariser is configured, thinking preferably off, 8,192-token output cap. The request asks for headed sections Goal, State (Done, In Progress, Blocked), Highlights, Next, Files; read and edited file lists are extracted from tool calls and forced into the summary; a previous summary is folded in. https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/context/agentic-compaction.ts#L52-L90 and https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/context/compaction-shared.ts#L759-L797 . Label: traced end to end. The summary request is its own request (a summariser prompt over the prefix), not a continuation of the cached conversation prefix; the findings do not say it reuses the cache.
- `basic` strategy (deterministic, no model call): keeps every typed user prompt, collapses dropped tool work into a `<SYSTEM_NOTICE>` summary, keeps the last 3 assistant texts verbatim, strips stale attachments, freezes earlier compaction output. https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/context/basic-compaction.ts#L452-L512 .
- Staged fallback: if `agentic` throws (not on abort), the code logs and runs `basic`. If the summariser returns no text (often because reasoning consumed the budget) or its input budget is exhausted, compaction is skipped and the notice says so. https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/context/compaction.ts#L568-L585 .
- Summariser window: only "its input budget is exhausted" is mentioned; no separate check of the summariser model's window against the history was described in the findings. Label: unchecked.
- Overflow recovery: a context-window error triggers one forced compaction through the same `prepareTurn` hook and a single retry, refused if the compacted request is not smaller. In overflow recovery `agentic` is never used (its own request could overflow); a custom compactor gets the first try but must return something strictly smaller and within target, else `basic` runs. https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/agents/src/agent-runtime.ts#L1378-L1423 ; must-shrink check https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/agents/src/agent-runtime.ts#L2254-L2306 ; compactor rule https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/context/compaction.ts#L495-L560 . Label: traced end to end (checked in review).
- Output-limit cut-offs: a turn cut at the output-token limit without a tool call gets up to 3 nudges, with one forced compaction first if available. A failed attempt that already streamed text or tool calls, or had provider-executed tool activity, is not retried.
- Foreign sessions imported from Claude Code, Codex or OpenCode are summarised once at resume by the same machinery. https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/context/compaction.ts#L725-L740

#### Tool-output pruning

- Executors cap command output at 48,000 characters (head and tail kept), reads at 2,000 lines, 2,000 characters per line, 48,000 characters per window; search at 48,000 per query. At request build, tool results over 8,000 characters (50,000 for `read` and `read_files` results and file attachments, corrected in review) are middle-truncated again with a notice naming a `cline://cache/<session>/<id>.result.txt` URI the model can page through with `read_files`. The cache is session-local, 16 MiB, evicts the oldest, drops an entry not read for 5 iterations. A cache miss returns "Cache not found ... DO NOT repeat side-effecting actions to recover output". An aggregate 6 MB text budget is a last-resort valve. https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/session/services/message-builder.ts#L28-L54 and https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/session/services/tool-result-cache.ts#L1-L78 . Label: read the definition (corrected in review).

#### Record in the log

- Compaction does not rewrite the transcript. The canonical messages file stays whole; the compacted working context is saved beside it as `<id>.compaction.json` with a hash of the source prefix so resume can tell whether the sidecar is stale. The sidecar is written atomically. A malformed sidecar is ignored ("Canonical history is unchanged; deleting the sidecar is safe"), so a failure midway leaves the full history. https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/session/models/session-compaction.ts#L22-L33 ; https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/session/stores/session-manifest-store.ts#L305-L312 ; https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/session/stores/session-manifest-store.ts#L290-L303 . Label: read the definition. A status notice with token counts before and after is emitted. `/newtask` is an alias of `/compact` in this tree.
- Messages are rewritten whole per session (a directory of JSON files plus a SQLite index).

#### Memory and session search

- Not found in the findings read: no memory store and no session search are described for Cline. `cline.safety.md` says compaction was not read. Label: not settled (no reviewer searched for memory).

### OpenHands

Source: `openhands.agents-sessions.md` (19 of 35 findings checked in review) and `openhands.safety.md`. Pinned commit `d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b`.

**What is and is not in the code read.** The repository `OpenHands/OpenHands` at this commit is only the Agent Canvas web client and its launchers (absence confirmed in review: top level, `src/`, `scripts/`, `docker/`, `helm/` listed; only one Python file, a tool shim). The agent loop, the condenser implementation (`LLMSummarizingCondenser`), the stuck detector, the base system prompt templates, tools, memory and event persistence live in `OpenHands/software-agent-sdk`, which was not cloned. Everything below is the client side: what Canvas configures, sends and displays. No compaction prompt, summariser model, size measurement, overflow recovery or pruning logic was available.

#### Prompt assembly (client side only)

- The server assembles the system prompt. Canvas names an `agent_profile_id` (the server resolves model, tools, instructions, MCP and secret scope) and may add `agent_launch_additions`: `system_message_suffix_append`, an `llm_profile_ref` override and extra `skills`. The suffix is built from three optional blocks: the runtime-services description (URLs reachable from the agent's sandbox), a "route at conversation start" instruction when a model router is attached, and the automation-setup instructions. Cloud ignores profile tools, instructions and skills for now. https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/agent-server-adapter.ts#L1146-L1185 . Label: read the definition (unchecked in review).
- The suffix blocks are free-form strings with no validation (the finding flags this as an injection channel if runtime-services info is attacker-influenced). The built-in prompts (`system_prompt.j2`, `security_policy.j2`) appear only by file name in a captured fixture; their text is not in this repository.
- Canvas-defined client tools (`canvas_ui_control`, `launch_child_conversation`, `request_secret`) carry their descriptions in `client_tools` of the start request. https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/agent-server-adapter.ts#L1217-L1231 . Label: checked in review (client tools finding).
- The session key is placed in the agent's environment and the system prompt tells the agent to use it (only when the automation service is advertised). Label: supported in review (safety file).
- Prompt cache strategy: not visible; not in this repository.

#### Compaction (client side only)

- Canvas stores a condenser section per agent profile (local) or per account (Cloud): `enabled` (default true) and `max_size` (client default 240, `max_size` counts events in the SDK condenser, per the findings' wording of "max_size" without a unit; the unit is not stated in the finding). The planner conversation is sent as a raw agent spec with an explicit `LLMSummarizingCondenser`, `max_size: 100`, `keep_first: 6`, and its own `usage_id` (`planning_condenser`) so its summaries are billed separately. https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/services/settings.ts#L16-L17 and https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/agent-server-adapter.ts#L1366-L1385 . Label: checked in review ("Condensers: supported", planner 100 / 6, client default 240, 2.5 s and 90 s).
- Manual compaction calls `POST /api/conversations/{id}/condense`, then waits for a `Condensation` event and a drop in `per_turn_token`: 2.5 s to let metrics settle, 90 s overall timeout, outcomes `compacted`, `no_change` or `timeout`; timeout is treated as failure, not no-op. This is a "check that it shrank" at the UI level. https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/conversation-service/agent-server-conversation-service.api.ts#L1120-L1146 and https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/hooks/use-await-context-compaction.ts#L1-L26 . Label: checked in review.
- The condenser settings page is Cloud-only, because local profiles own their condenser. The trigger rule (events versus tokens) and the server's effective default are not in this repository.

#### Record in the log

- A `Condensation` event carries `forgotten_event_ids`, an optional `summary` and `summary_offset` (where the summary is inserted in the resulting view). The log keeps every event and the model's view is a derived projection. https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/types/agent-server/core/events/condensation-event.ts#L5-L27 . Label: read the definition. The event union also has `SystemPrompt`, `Condensation` request and summary types. https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/types/agent-server/core/openhands-event.ts#L25-L46
- What a failure midway leaves: not visible (server side).

#### Instruction files and skills

- Skills: the public catalog is baked into the web bundle (`@openhands/extensions`) and sent in `agent_context.skills` with keyword triggers; `load_public_skills: false`; user and project skills still come from the server (`load_user_skills`, `load_project_skills: true`). Keyword matching runs in the SDK. Enablement is an allow-list for the catalog and a deny-list for user and project skills. A slash command at the start of a message attaches its skill even if disabled. https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/agent-server-adapter.ts#L866-L955 . Label: traced for the client; not checked in review.
- Microagents survive only as a legacy path marker (`.openhands/microagents/`, treated as a pre-rename skills directory; scope derived from path strings). https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/utils/skill-scope.ts#L11-L19 . Label: read the definition.
- Hooks: `<project_dir>/.openhands/hooks.json` is loaded by the server on request (5 s timeout, no parent walk) and shipped in `hook_config`. No trust prompt (the only prompt is a checkbox for `/launch?plugins=` deep links); a failed load starts the conversation with no hooks (fails open). https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/hooks-service.ts#L17-L58 . Label: checked in review (supported).
- Discovery order, size budget of `AGENTS.md` or other instruction files: not in this repository.

#### Memory and session search

- Not in this repository. The findings list "the memory feature" as SDK, and the review notes memory was "not checked". History search over events is a client of the server: `limit` capped at 100 per page, timestamp filters, `TIMESTAMP_DESC` sort. https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/event-service/event-service.types.ts#L10-L36 . Label: read the definition.

### Zed

Source: `zed.md` (one file; 17 of 27 findings checked in review). Pinned commit `3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd`. The Zed Agent runs inside the editor process as a GPUI entity (`Thread`); compaction is checked inside the per-request loop. The findings cover compaction and tool-output limits only for this group. System prompt assembly, instruction files (rules files) and memory were not in the scope of the study (the scope line lists checkpoints, tool permissions, sandboxing, the ACP client, edit tools, compaction); a keyword search of the file finds nothing for them.

#### Prompt cache

- The compaction request keeps the tools in the request with `tool_choice: none` when the same model is used, to keep the cached prefix; with a different compaction model thinking is turned off. https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/thread.rs#L4764-L4835 . Label: traced end to end (checked in review for the compaction constants; the cache-prefix claim itself was not separately listed).

#### Compaction

- Trigger: before every model request in the turn loop (`compaction_message_target_ix`). It compares the last reported usage (`input + cache creation + cache read + output` tokens) with a threshold computed from the model's input capacity, in one of three forms: a percentage (default 90%), tokens used, or tokens remaining. Capacity is `min(input_limit, combined_limit - output_limit)`. Auto-compaction is enabled only when capacity is at least 80,000 tokens; smaller models get a UI warning instead. https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/thread.rs#L2959-L2990 ; threshold forms https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/thread.rs#L5004-L5028 ; defaults https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/assets/settings/default.json#L1294-L1306 . Label: traced end to end; constants (80,000 minimum window, 90% default) supported in review.
- Size measurement: the provider-reported usage of the previous request, not an estimate. The check runs once per loop iteration on the previous request's usage, so one very large tool result can overshoot the window before compaction can act. Label: checked in review.
- Summariser: the model set in `agent.compaction_model`, else the thread's own model. The request is the whole history so far plus a fixed prompt asking for a handoff (goal, state, context, next steps, pitfalls). https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent_settings/src/prompts/compaction_prompt.txt#L1-L10 . No check that the compaction model's window fits the history was described. Label: checked in review (trigger and model); window check unchecked.
- Kept verbatim: the last 80,000 bytes (about 20k tokens) of earlier user messages are re-sent before the summary, the oldest one truncated to fit; agent messages and tool results are dropped. A trailing user message with no reply is kept out of the summary. https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/thread.rs#L5286-L5315 . Label: checked in review (80,000-byte carry-forward supported).
- Record in the log: the summary is inserted as a `Message::Compaction(Summary)`; all earlier messages stay in the thread for the UI but are no longer sent. `CompactionInfo::ProviderNative` stores provider-side compaction items for providers that compact server-side. https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/thread.rs#L3412-L3518 and https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/thread.rs#L224-L231 . Label: traced end to end.
- Failure: a provider error during compaction follows the same retry and backoff as a normal completion error. An empty summary raises an error (nothing is inserted). Manual `/compact` forces the same path regardless of usage. https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/thread.rs#L2708-L2790 .
- Overflow recovery: none found. A `PromptTooLarge` rejection only marks the usage indicator "exceeded" (`mark_token_limit_exceeded`) and is not in the transient-error list, so it surfaces as an error. https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/thread.rs#L2469-L2484 . Label: inferred (checked in review as "inferences kept as inferences"; the review did not list this one as confirmed absence).
- Check that compaction shrank the context: not described.

#### Tool-output pruning

- None as a separate stage; compaction is the only mechanism (found in the code read, stated in the finding; not marked absence confirmed). The terminal tool caps output returned to the model at 16 KiB unless selection mode removes the cap; the schema exposes `head_lines` and `tail_lines`. https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tools/terminal_tool.rs#L25-L25 . Label: read the definition; not checked in review ("output limits and retry" listed as not checked).

#### Instruction files and memory

- Not covered by the study. Nothing found in the file for rules files, memory or session search.

### Warp

Source: `warp.md` (16 of 29 findings checked in review). Pinned commit `4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39`.

**What is and is not in the code read.** The agent loop is not in this repository (absence confirmed in review: the only model call is `POST .../multi-agent` through `warp_multi_agent_client`; no Anthropic, OpenAI or Google API host in any `.rs` under `app/` or `crates/`). The server holds the system prompt, tool descriptions, model calls, model routing and summarisation. The client is a tool executor, UI and policy gate. The wire schema is a separate repo (`warpdotdev/warp-proto-apis`), not read. So system prompt text, compaction, summariser, trigger and pruning are not in the code read; the study's section 6 lists them as "server-side".

#### What the client does show

- Each model turn is one request containing task context, input, model ids, capability flags and a `supported_tools` list; tool availability is decided client-side from session type (local, remote SSH host, none) and feature flags, and is advertised on every request. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/agent/api/impl.rs#L65-L121 and https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/agent/api/impl.rs#L225-L303 . Label: traced end to end (loop-is-server-side finding checked in review as absence confirmed).
- Tool descriptions for MCP are not deferred: per request the client copies every active server's name, description, tools and resources into `mcp_context` (grouped by server when `MCPGroupedServerContext`). No deferral, search or code-mode layer in the client. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/agent/api.rs#L267-L345 . Label: read the definition; not checked in review (MCP trust listed as not checked).
- Overflow: `ContextWindowExceeded` is surfaced as an error, not handled by client-side compaction. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/controller.rs#L4108-L4120 . Label: unchecked as a single claim; the finding's wider statement that no client-side compaction exists rests on the loop being server-side (confirmed). Server may compact on its side: not visible.
- Retry budget `MAX_RECOVERY_ATTEMPTS = 3` (supported in review).
- Local persistence: SQLite via diesel, with an `agent_conversations` table and a migration `add_summary_to_agent_conversations` (the findings mention the migration name only; what the summary column holds is not described). https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/persistence/migrations/2026-07-07-000000_add_summary_to_agent_conversations/up.sql#L1-L1 . Label: unchecked.
- Redaction: a client regex pass on outbound inputs (user query, context, attachments, command output, file contents, search results); off by default (`safe_mode_enabled` default false; supported in review). Relay inputs from other agents are exempt.
- Codebase indexing uploads raw code fragments (4,000,000-byte batches) to Warp's server for embedding, with no redaction (supported in review). This is the nearest thing to a retrieval or memory store in the code read.
- Instruction files (for example rules files), memory and session search: not described in the findings for Warp.

### OpenCodex

Source: `opencodex.md` (one file; 18 of 38 findings checked in review; the compaction finding, number 28, was not among them). Pinned commit `ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396`. OpenCodex is a gateway that translates between Anthropic Messages, Chat Completions and OpenAI Responses wires and routes to many providers. It has no agent loop of its own; it has three bounded loops inside a request (web search, compaction summariser, vision description). The client harness (for example Codex) owns the history, the system prompt, instruction files and memory, so none of those are in this code.

#### Prompt assembly and cache

- Nothing assembled here: the gateway forwards the client's request. The Messages handler converts the Anthropic body into a Responses body and calls `handleResponses` in process; Chat Completions does the same. https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/server/claude-messages.ts#L1240-L1262 . Label: traced end to end for Messages (unchecked in review).
- Prompt-cache strategy: not described in the findings.

#### Compaction

- Two paths, by target. Native Responses targets: `/v1/responses/compact` is forwarded and the reply buffered up to 32 MiB (`COMPACT_RESPONSE_MAX_BYTES`) under the stall deadline. Routed (non-OpenAI) models: the gateway appends `{type: "compaction_trigger"}` to the input and runs an internal Responses turn whose last message is `COMPACT_PROMPT` (a short "context checkpoint compaction" handoff prompt), with structured-output format removed and, when needed, an extra "Return only the handoff summary. Do not call tools" sentence. The turn goes through the same `handleResponses` pipeline, so it uses the same send budget and routing. https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/server/responses/compact.ts#L243-L243 ; https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/server/responses/compact.ts#L1777-L1802 ; https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/server/responses/compact.ts#L1895-L1914 . Label: traced end to end for the routed path; not checked in review.
- Validation ("check the result"): `guardCompactionSummary` converts any tool-call attempt, empty text or abnormal stop into a 502 `compaction_invalid_summary`. The caller then demands exactly one `compaction` item with a non-empty `ocx1:` envelope; the summary becomes replacement history items. The code comment says the internal turn answers 200 even when it failed, so the body is inspected; reporting a failure is preferred to installing "(no summary available)" as history (issue #422). A thrown retained-compaction error becomes 502 `invalid_compaction_summary`. https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/responses/compaction-summary.ts#L6-L45 and https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/server/responses/core-pipeline.ts#L86-L142 . Label: traced; Round 1 "tool-free summariser" confirmed. This validates structure, not that the context shrank; no size check is described.
- Quota failures on a native compact fall back to a remembered handoff model. Label: unchecked.
- Trigger, numbers, size measurement and what is kept verbatim: not here; the calling client decides when to compact and what to send.
- Overflow recovery in the gateway sense: in combo failover, a provider-target or definite context overflow hops to the next target, with cooldown scope `none` (request-shape failures do not cool a target); `context_length_exceeded` is also listed as a stop in the generic status list, so the finding's wording distinguishes the two cases (provider-target context overflow hops; the generic code stops). https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/combos/failover.ts#L1177-L1230 and https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/combos/failover.ts#L992-L1021 . Label: unchecked in review (the Round 1 row below is marked corrected in the file). This reroutes to another model; it does not shrink the context.

#### Pruning, log, instruction files, memory

- Tool-output pruning: none (the gateway has no history of its own). Per-turn byte budgets exist: 256 MiB body budget, 512 MiB, 256 active turns (supported in review).
- Record in the log: none; no session log. Telemetry and request logs refuse to echo configured credentials.
- Instruction files and memory: none in this code. Remote Workspace hosts an agent only by spawning the Codex app-server in a throwaway `mkdtemp` directory (mode 0700) on the hub; its instructions and memory are Codex's.

## 3. Comparison

Cells are short; the account in section 2 has the detail and the permalinks. "n/d" means the input files do not describe it; "not in repo" means the code that was read does not hold it.

### 3.1 Compaction: trigger, measurement, summariser

| Project | Trigger and numbers | How size is measured | Summariser model | Window check for the summariser |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Codex | `min(config, 0.9 x window)`; pre-turn, mid-turn, post-turn (off by default); keeps user messages up to 20,000 tokens; remote V2 keeps up to 64,000 | n/d (status over active tokens; optional prefix baseline) | Same turn model, no tools; OpenAI and Azure use an opaque server-side item | n/d; downshift compacts on the previous model first |
| OpenCode | Before each step at `window - max(15% of window, 16k)` (16k floor only for windows of 32k or more); keeps newest 15,000 tokens; unknown window means it never fires | Last provider-measured count plus length/4 for the tail; images 1,500, PDFs 2,000 | Same model; `agents.compaction.model` overrides | None described; request is fitted to size by a ladder |
| OpenClaw | After each response at `min(window - reserve, 0.75 x window)`; reserve `max(configured, 20k)` capped at 25% of window; keeps 20,000 | Provider usage; estimate from last good usage after an error | Session model; `compaction.model` unless model-locked; fallback chain | None found |
| Hermes Agent | `(window - max_tokens) x 0.50`, floor 0.75 under 512K, cap 85% of usable window; lean tail 2.5% of window clamped 10K..25K | Provider prompt tokens; rough estimate before first response; 413 scored in serialised bytes | `compression` auxiliary slot (main model if unset); 300 s timeout floor | Feasibility probe; trigger lowered to the summariser window; hard error below a minimum |
| Oh My Pi | Window minus max(15% of window, 16,384), or a percentage, or fixed; keep 20,000; summary cap 16,384; 0.8 hysteresis; mid-turn on | Token count floored by a local estimate of stored conversation | `compactionModel`, current, role models, then largest-context model | Handled by selection; no explicit check |
| Pi | `tokens > window - 16,384`; keep about 20,000 | Last provider usage plus chars/4; usage older than the last compaction ignored; image = 4,800 chars | Session's own model | None; no input-size guard |
| DeepSeek harness | `min(0.8 x window, window - reserved output - 65,536)` before every step; keep 16% of message budget | Token meter over the logged request header (route-specific) | Conversation's own route unless overridden | Window of the routed model must be known; model switch compacts first or refuses |
| goose | `min(0.8 x window, 225,000)` before every inference; no verbatim tail, last user text only | Last provider `total_tokens` plus estimate; tiktoken `o200k_base` if no usage | Session model, thinking off, cache writes off | Staged tool-result removal (0, 10, 20, 50, 100%) if the summariser overflows |
| Cline | Before each request at 90% of input limit (about 81% of window when only the window is known); target 70% of trigger; keep last 20,000 estimated tokens; off in core, on in CLI | About 3 chars/token plus images, scaled down by the provider-count ratio (cap 4x); unknown limit means no auto compaction (128,000 fallback for manual and overflow) | Session model unless configured; thinking off; 8,192-token output | n/d; only "input budget exhausted" skips |
| OpenHands | Client `max_size` 240 (planner 100, `keep_first` 6); trigger unit not in repo | not in repo | Planner condenser has its own usage id; rest not in repo | not in repo |
| Zed | Before every request at 90% (or tokens used or remaining) of capacity; auto only if capacity is at least 80,000; keep last 80,000 bytes of user messages | Provider usage of the previous request | `agent.compaction_model`, else thread model | None found |
| Warp | not in repo (server) | not in repo | not in repo | not in repo |
| OpenCodex | The calling client decides; gateway only forwards or runs the summary turn | None | The routed target model | None |

### 3.2 Compaction: request shape, fallbacks, shrink check, overflow, log

| Project | Summary request reuses the main prefix? | Staged fallback | Check that it shrank | Context-length error | Record in the log and failure midway |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Codex | Full history plus one appended user message, no tools (cache intent not stated, inferred) | Oldest-item drop inside compaction | n/d | In compaction: drop oldest and retry; in sampling: mark full, end turn; guardian: one compact and retry | Compaction item with `replacement_history`; atomic replace; failure leaves history unchanged |
| OpenCode | Yes by design: same system, tools, history plus a final user message | `deliver` ladder: unchanged, history minus tail, flattened transcript clipped over five levels; shrink to 85/70/50/35% on "too long" | Not described (the ladder checks the input, not the output) | Compact once per step then restart the step; no recovery after output started | Message with `keepID`; `Compaction.Ended` advances the instruction epoch; failed auto compaction fails the step |
| OpenClaw | n/d | Safeguard: audit and regenerate (max 3), then structured fallback marked degraded | Quality audit of content, not size | Pre-exits, up to 3 compactions, one tool-result truncation pass | Transcript generation with reason `compaction` (inferred); failure state not described |
| Hermes Agent | No (single user message) | Failure ladder: retry on main model, cooldowns, abort-and-preserve for terminal errors, else deterministic summary | "Ineffective" breaker after two confirmed no-shrink compactions | 413: compress, strip images, retry; "too long": adopt provider limit; "output cap": clamp; max 3 | In place on the same session id; old rows `active=0, compacted=1`; abort leaves history unchanged |
| Oh My Pi | n/d | Method chain `remote, snapcompact, handoff, shake, soft`, each failure falls to the next | Yes: a summary that would not reduce context, or a shake that reclaims too little, falls through | Drop failed turn; optional model promotion; else compaction with reason `overflow` | `compaction` entry with `firstKeptEntryId`, `tokensBefore`, `method`; nothing deleted |
| Pi | No (`cacheRetention: "none"`, serialised text) | None | None | One compact-and-retry per run; failed attempt hidden by `context_edit`; second failure stops | `compaction` entry; old entries kept; failure leaves the log untouched except omission edits |
| DeepSeek harness | Yes: same system prompt, tools and shadowed messages, instruction as final user message | Prune, then compact with zero retention, then retry | Yes: summary rejected unless its framed estimate is strictly smaller than the span | `CONTEXT_WINDOW_EXCEEDED`: prune, compact with zero retention, retry (1 per idle period) | `compaction/start`, `summary`, replacing `user/message`, `end`; failure appends `end` with error; orphan blocks until `session/end-seed` |
| goose | No (flattened transcript in a system prompt) | Tool-result removal inside the summary request | n/d (absence not confirmed) | Hide error, compact, rerun; up to 3 per turn | Old rows kept, `agent_visible` flipped; summary row flagged |
| Cline | No (its own summariser request) | `agentic` then deterministic `basic`; overflow uses only `basic` or a strictly smaller custom compactor | Yes: retry refused unless the request shrank | One forced compaction and one retry | Transcript untouched; compacted view in sidecar `<id>.compaction.json` with source-prefix hash |
| OpenHands | not in repo | not in repo | UI waits for a token drop after manual condense | not in repo | `Condensation` event; log keeps everything |
| Zed | Yes when the same model: tools kept with `tool_choice: none` | None | n/d | None (error shown) | `Message::Compaction(Summary)` inserted; older messages stay for UI |
| Warp | not in repo | not in repo | not in repo | Client shows `ContextWindowExceeded` as an error | Local `agent_conversations` summary column (contents unknown) |
| OpenCodex | The internal turn goes through the normal pipeline | None | Structure only (non-empty, no tool calls) | Combo failover hops to the next target | None |

### 3.3 Prompt assembly, cache strategy, pruning

| Project | Where prompt text lives | How dynamic state is kept out of the cached part | Old tool output |
|---|---|---|---|
| Codex | Per-model `instructions_template` inside the bundled `models.json`; sections in code | Per-section snapshots; only changed sections rendered as diffs into history; world state persisted in the rollout | Middle-cut at record time (10,000 tokens); never pruned later |
| OpenCode | `system.txt` (15 lines) plus per-model overlay files chosen by substring | Keyed instruction sources: frozen baseline in `system[1]`, changes appended as system messages, frozen in durable events; re-based at compaction | None; call-time cut (2,000 lines or 50 KiB, full text on disk 7 days); images bounded |
| OpenClaw | Code builders, no templates | Everything volatile placed below `SYSTEM_PROMPT_CACHE_BOUNDARY`; prefix cached by hash | Live cap 30% of window; optional cache-TTL soft trim then hard clear, frozen and recorded |
| Hermes Agent | Code plus data in `agent/`; three tiers | Stable, context, volatile tiers built once and rebuilt only at compaction; four Anthropic breakpoints; review fork reuses parent bytes | Model-free projection at compaction; call-time spill (100K chars, 1,500-char preview) |
| Oh My Pi | Handlebars templates | Static blocks first, one trailing `<project-context>` block; pruning waits for a cold cache | `supersedeReads`, `dropUseless` each turn, cache-aware; snapcompact caps results at 2,000 chars |
| Pi | Code sections; tool snippet next to each tool | Prompt and tool changes are system-message patches appended to the transcript | None between compactions; per-tool caps (2,000 lines, 50 KB) |
| DeepSeek harness | Ordered named sections from a table, per-deployment YAML | Dynamic facts in a per-step `runtime-context` user message, appended only when changed; system prompt re-committed only on change | Model-free prune of results over 8,192 chars; spill over 12,500 tokens |
| goose | `system.md` template (39 lines) plus keyed extras sorted by name | Volatile data in a persisted trailing `<turn-context>` message | Spill over 200,000 chars; optional LLM tool-pair summaries (off) |
| Cline | One template with slots; per-model difference only in the tool list | Mode tag on each user message; stale-read rewrites batched at 64 KB | Caps at executor and at request build with a recoverable `cline://cache` URI |
| OpenHands | Server (not in repo) | not in repo | not in repo |
| Zed | not in the study | Only the compaction request keeps tools to reuse the prefix | None separate; terminal capped at 16 KiB |
| Warp | Server (not in repo) | not in repo | not in repo |
| OpenCodex | Client | not applicable | None |

### 3.4 Instruction files

| Project | Files and order | Budget | Trust |
|---|---|---|---|
| Codex | Global `AGENTS.override.md` or `AGENTS.md`; then root to cwd, one file per directory (`AGENTS.override.md`, `AGENTS.md`, fallbacks) | 32 KiB combined, warn on cut; global file separate; thread-level 10,000 tokens, rejected not cut | Untrusted project returns nothing; root markers only from non-project config layers |
| OpenCode | Global `AGENTS.md`, ancestors to project root, `instructions` globs and URLs; nested files injected after `read` | None found | No project trust prompt found; remote URL must be listed in config |
| OpenClaw | Six workspace files: `AGENTS.md, SOUL.md, IDENTITY.md, USER.md, BOOTSTRAP.md, MEMORY.md` | 20,000 each, 60,000 total; `AGENTS.md` cut with a policy digest | Per-session-type filter (`MEMORY.md` dropped for group, channel, sub-agent, cron); no trust gate described |
| Hermes Agent | First type wins: `.hermes.md`, `AGENTS.md` chain, `CLAUDE.md`, cursor rules | 20,000 chars per file or 6% of window (20K..500K); head 70%, tail 20% | Regex threat scan; a hit replaces a repo context file with `[BLOCKED]` |
| Oh My Pi | Many harness formats by priority, one file per depth, paragraph dedupe, `@path` imports | None reported | No project trust gate (`isProjectTrusted()` always true) |
| Pi | Global, then every ancestor to the filesystem root; one of five names per directory | None | Context files load regardless of project trust |
| DeepSeek harness | `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `.local` variants; user-global then root to cwd; delivered as user messages | 64 KiB per chain, 1 MiB per file | No trust gate in code read |
| goose | `.goosehints`, `AGENTS.md`; global then git root to cwd; `@` imports | Main file uncapped; imports depth 3, 1 MiB | No trust prompt; Unicode tag strip only |
| Cline | Workspace `AGENTS.md`, `.clinerules`, `.cline/rules`, global `AGENTS.md`, global dirs | None found | No trust prompt for workspace plugins and hooks |
| OpenHands | Server (not in repo); skills from a bundle plus server; `.openhands/hooks.json` | not in repo | No prompt for hooks; fails open |
| Zed, Warp, OpenCodex | not covered | not covered | not covered |

### 3.5 Memory

| OpenCode | No memory feature found | n/a | n/a | n/a | n/a | None found |
| OpenClaw | `MEMORY.md`, `memory/*.md`, short-term recall | `MEMORY.md` 10,000 chars | Model tools, flush turn before compaction, nightly dreaming job | None | 25% removal guard on consolidation; Skill Workshop changes versioned and undoable | `memory_search` hybrid vector and keyword |
| Hermes Agent | `MEMORY.md` 2,200 chars, `USER.md` 1,375 chars; agent-written skills | As stated | Chat model; background review fork; curator | Off by default; when on: inline prompt or staging queue; background review always staged | Curator ledger with before/after hashes, per-entry rollback, tarball snapshots | FTS5 `session_search`, no LLM |

## 4. Patterns and trade-offs

### 4.1 Keeping dynamic state out of the cached prompt

Four approaches exist.
1. **Volatile text below a boundary or in tiers.** OpenClaw (`SYSTEM_PROMPT_CACHE_BOUNDARY`, prefix cached by hash), Hermes (stable, context and volatile tiers, rebuilt only at compaction), Oh My Pi (one trailing `<project-context>` block). Cheap and simple. It does not tell the model that something changed mid-session, and a changed fact either waits for the next rebuild (Hermes: a new skill or memory write reaches the prompt at the next compaction or session) or breaks the prefix.
2. **A per-step or per-turn tail message.** goose (`<turn-context>`, persisted so the turn reuses identical bytes, skipped under a 32,000-token limit), DeepSeek harness (`runtime-context` user message appended only when the rendered text differs), Cline (mode tag on each user message). The prefix never moves. Each approach needs a rule for what happens to old snapshots at compaction: goose carries the latest forward; DeepSeek's findings do not say.
3. **Keyed sources with a baseline and diffs written into the history.** OpenCode (hash delta and already-rendered text frozen in a durable event; first observation rendered into `system[1]`; later changes become system messages; baseline re-based at compaction), Codex (typed sections with persisted snapshots and JSON merge-patch; fresh windows render everything), Pi (system-prompt sections patched by system messages appended to the transcript; tool additions and removals likewise). This is the only approach that makes "the model has seen X" a recorded fact, and the rendered text is stored so replay does not re-render. Cost: more code and special cases (Codex has legacy-fragment matchers; OpenCode refuses to start a session whose unavailable source was never initialised).
4. **Prompt text as data per model.** Codex (catalog row), OpenCode (overlay files chosen by model-id substring, no provider check, so a gateway-served model with "gpt" in its id gets the GPT prompt), Hermes (guidance gated on model-name substrings), Cline (one template; only the tool list differs per model). Pi and goose have no per-model prompt branching.

A related risk: Hermes and Oh My Pi note that tool descriptions can change the tool bytes, so Hermes' review fork inherits the parent's tool list and restricts only at dispatch. Codex rebuilds the tool list every step from (model row, flags, environment).

### 4.2 Compaction trigger

Three formulas. A fraction of the window: Codex 0.9, goose 0.8 capped at 225,000, Zed 0.9 of capacity, DeepSeek 0.8 with a 65,536-token headroom floor, Hermes 0.50 raised to 0.75 for windows under 512K (a hard cap at 85%). Window minus a reserve: OpenCode (15% or 16k), Pi (16,384), Oh My Pi (15% or 16,384), OpenClaw (reserve of at least 20k, also capped at 75%). A fraction of the input limit: Cline (0.9, which is about 0.81 of the window when only the window is known). The numbers differ by a factor of two (Hermes at 50% against Codex at 90%); no project in the findings shows a measurement that favours one. All check before the next request; Pi also checks after each tool batch, and Oh My Pi and Codex also mid-turn. When the window is unknown: OpenCode's ceiling becomes infinite (never fires, overflow only), Cline skips auto compaction (128,000 for manual and overflow), DeepSeek warns once and continues uncompacted.

### 4.3 Measuring context size

Anchor on the provider's last reported count and add an estimate for what came after: OpenCode, Pi, goose, Zed (usage only), Hermes, OpenClaw. The estimate is characters over 3 or 4. Corrections exist in four forms: Cline scales its budget down by the ratio of the provider's real count to its estimate (cap 4x); Pi ignores usage recorded before the latest compaction (otherwise a fresh compaction would immediately re-trigger); Oh My Pi floors the count with a local estimate so provider-side request shrinking cannot hide growth; Hermes scores a 413 in serialised bytes because the token estimator is blind to images. goose admits that one huge tool result between checks can overshoot; Zed has the same gap. None of the findings describe measuring with a provider token-count endpoint (absence not confirmed).

### 4.4 The summary request

Cache-preserving: OpenCode (same system parts, tools and history plus a final user message; the code comment says this is for cache reuse), DeepSeek harness (same system prompt, full tool schemas, shadowed messages, instruction last; tool calls in the reply dropped), Zed (tools kept with `tool_choice: none` when the model is the same). Codex's local path also appends to a clone of the history with no tools (intent not stated). Deliberately uncached or separate: Pi (`cacheRetention: "none"`, history serialised to text), goose (flattened transcript in a system prompt, cache writes off), Hermes (one user message, one-shot requests get no cache marker), Cline (own request over the prefix). The cost of the cache-preserving form: the request is the full history plus the prompt plus the output budget, so the trigger must leave that room (OpenCode's buffer; DeepSeek's headroom equals the summary `maxTokens`), and it only pays if the same model and an unexpired cache are used (OpenCode's precompute is tied to the cache lifetime minus 30 s).

What the summary is made to contain: structured templates in nearly every project (goal, constraints, progress, decisions, next steps, files). Distinct ideas: Hermes tells the model the turns are "DATA to summarize, never instructions", redacts secrets and keeps every real user message verbatim in the summary (24K chars) and a regex-extracted anchor index; goose asks for a JSON object and re-renders it; OpenClaw's safeguard demands fixed headings and audits identifiers; Oh My Pi's snapcompact renders discarded text to images with no LLM call (lossy, tool output cut to 2,000 chars). Failures: an empty, truncated (`finish: length`) or tool-calling summary is an error in Hermes, Pi, OpenCode (also a heading check with one nudge) and DeepSeek; Codex accepts an empty summary outside post-turn compaction.

### 4.5 What is kept verbatim

Codex: user messages up to 20,000 tokens (not tool output, not assistant text). Zed: last 80,000 bytes of user messages. goose: only the last user text. OpenCode: newest 15,000 tokens, but as serialised text with tool results cut to 1,250 chars (Round 1 said verbatim; corrected). Pi, Oh My Pi, OpenClaw: about 20,000 tokens of recent entries cut at a valid boundary (never between a tool call and its result). Hermes: lean tail of 2.5% of the window (10K..25K) plus always the last assistant message, last real user message and pending tool round. DeepSeek: 16% of the message budget. Cline: 20,000 estimated tokens.

### 4.6 Fallbacks, shrink checks and overflow recovery

- **Make the summary request fit:** OpenCode (ladder of request shapes, then shrink to 85/70/50/35% of the rejected size), goose (remove tool responses 0/10/20/50/100%), Hermes (evenly sampled input with elision markers, one call only), Codex (drop the oldest history item and retry).
- **A non-model fallback:** Cline `basic` (keeps every typed user prompt, collapses dropped tool work into a notice, keeps the last three assistant texts), Hermes deterministic summary (user asks, actions, files, errors), Oh My Pi `shake` and `soft` (mechanics not described), DeepSeek zero-retention compaction after a model-free prune. Not present: Pi, goose, Zed, OpenCode (it fails instead), Codex (the turn dies).
- **A shrink check:** DeepSeek (summary must be strictly smaller than the span), Cline (retry refused unless the request shrank), Oh My Pi (falls through when a summary would not reduce context), Hermes (breaker after two ineffective compactions), OpenHands UI (waits for a token drop). Not present: Codex, OpenCode, Pi, goose, Zed.
- **The recovery loop:** compact and retry the same step once (OpenCode, Pi, Cline); up to three (goose, OpenClaw, Hermes); drop the failed turn first (Pi `context_edit`, Oh My Pi); mark usage full and end the turn (Codex sampling path); only reroute (OpenCodex combo failover, which does not shrink anything); nothing (Zed). Distinct signals handled by Hermes: 413 body-size versus "prompt too long" versus "output cap too large", each with its own action, and adopting a provider-reported limit but never a guessed one.
- **Abort and preserve:** Hermes aborts compression on terminal network, truncation, empty-content and auth failures rather than replacing the middle with a placeholder; Cline ignores a malformed sidecar; Pi writes nothing on failure.

### 4.7 Tool-output pruning

Three families. Nothing between compactions, only a cut when the result is produced: Codex, Pi, Zed, OpenCode (V2). A cut at production with the full text kept where the model can fetch it: OpenCode (disk for 7 days), Hermes (spill with a 1,500-char preview, aggregate 200,000 chars per turn), Cline (`cline://cache` URI, 16 MiB), DeepSeek (spill store; fails open to inline), goose (200,000-char temp file). A pass that shrinks old results later: DeepSeek (model-free prune over 8,192 chars, run first under pressure), Hermes (at compaction), Oh My Pi (`supersedeReads` and `dropUseless` every turn, waiting for a cold cache), OpenClaw (cache-TTL soft trim then hard clear, frozen and recorded so a restart rebuilds the same prefix; off except for Anthropic), Cline (stale re-reads rewritten, batched at 64 KB), goose (LLM one-line tool-pair summaries, off by default). The cache-aware ones (Oh My Pi, OpenClaw, Cline) exist because rewriting old bytes breaks the prefix cache.

### 4.8 Recording compaction in the log

- **A new entry on a branch of a tree; nothing deleted:** Pi (`compaction` entry with `firstKeptEntryId`, `tokensBefore`, snapshot of the system prompt and tool state), Oh My Pi (also `method`, `preserveData`).
- **A complete replacement base in an append-only file:** Codex (`replacement_history` item; resume replays only the suffix).
- **Events around a replace operation:** DeepSeek (`compaction/start`, `summary`, replacing `user/message` citing shadowed seqs, `compaction/end`; a start with no end blocks further compaction until a later `session/end-seed`; if the summary committed but the close failed the surface is replaced while the lock stays open).
- **A flag flip on existing rows:** goose (`agent_visible`), Hermes (`active=0, compacted=1`, still searchable, same session id; a config comment names a "session-rotation bug cluster" as the reason it left the child-session design).
- **A pointer message plus an epoch:** OpenCode (`keepID`, instruction baselines re-based).
- **A sidecar:** Cline (`<id>.compaction.json` with a hash of the source prefix; stale or malformed sidecars are ignored).
- **A condensation event:** OpenHands (`forgotten_event_ids`, `summary`, `summary_offset`).

### 4.9 Instruction files

Discovery: global then root-to-cwd (Codex, goose, DeepSeek, Hermes for the `AGENTS.md` chain); global then every ancestor to the filesystem root (Pi, no repository boundary); nearest-wins per depth with a priority table across harness formats (Oh My Pi); fixed workspace file set (OpenClaw); first matching type wins (Hermes). Delivery: system prompt (Pi, goose, OpenClaw, Hermes), user-role message (Codex, DeepSeek), keyed instruction source with diffs (OpenCode). Nested files after a `read`: OpenCode (ledger is synthetic messages in history, so they are re-injected after compaction), DeepSeek (on `read`, `write`, `edit`). Budgets: Codex 32 KiB combined; OpenClaw 20,000 per file and 60,000 total with a digest of must/never lines; Hermes 20,000 or 6% of window; DeepSeek 64 KiB per chain; none in Pi, OpenCode, goose (main file), Cline, Oh My Pi. Trust: only Codex (untrusted project returns nothing) and Hermes (regex scan) gate project instruction files; Pi gates `.pi/` resources but not context files; goose, Cline, Oh My Pi, DeepSeek and OpenHands have no gate for the content they read. After compaction: OpenCode re-bases; Codex fresh windows render everything; DeepSeek's compaction path does not re-inject (nested files re-project when touched); goose subdirectory hints drop out until the model touches those paths again (inferred).

### 4.10 Memory

- **Chat model writes directly:** goose (prompt-only "confirm with the user"), OpenClaw (workspace files via ordinary tools plus a flush turn before compaction), Hermes (`memory` tool, 2,200 and 1,375 chars).
- **Background model writes:** Codex (two phases, consolidation agent in a locked profile, no approval), OpenClaw dreaming (score threshold, 25% removal guard), Oh My Pi (Hindsight, Mnemopi, Sharpshooter), Hermes background review.
- **A person approves:** Hermes only, and off by default (`memory.write_approval`, `skills.write_approval`).
- **A ledger and rollback:** Hermes only (skills); OpenClaw Skill Workshop is "versioned and undoable" (not traced).
- **No memory:** OpenCode, Pi, Cline, DeepSeek (absence confirmed for DeepSeek only).

Session search exists in Hermes (FTS5, no LLM, compaction-archived rows still found), OpenClaw (hybrid vector and keyword over memory files), DeepSeek (opt-in, in-memory in the web profile).
